   205	     ↓
   206	Tenant B AI training data
   207	```
   208	
   209	unless explicitly permitted through a future anonymized shared-learning mechanism.
   210	
   211	---
   212	
   213	## 6. Multi-Tenant Data Model
   214	
   215	All tenant-owned entities must include:
   216	
   217	```text
   218	tenant_id
   219	```
   220	
   221	Where relevant, entities should also contain:
   222	
   223	```text
   224	portfolio_id
   225	broker_account_id
   226	strategy_id
   227	strategy_version_id
   228	```
   229	
   230	Examples:
   231	
   232	```text
   233	orders
   234	trades
   235	positions
   236	signals
   237	strategy_allocations
   238	risk_decisions
   239	backtest_runs
   240	ai_decisions
   241	portfolios
   242	broker_accounts
   243	strategy_configs
   244	audit_events
   245	```
   246	
   247	All must be tenant scoped.
   248	
   249	---
   250	
   251	## 7. Tenant Entity
   252	
   253	A tenant should contain:
   254	
   255	```text
   256	tenant_id
   257	name
   258	status
   259	subscription_plan
   260	created_at
   261	default_currency
   262	risk_profile
   263	resource_limits
   264	features_enabled
   265	```
   610	Curated strategies available to many tenants.
   611	
   612	Example:
   613	
   614	```text
   615	momentum-v3
   616	breakout-v2
   617	mean-reversion-v4
   618	```
   619	
   620	### Tenant-owned strategies
   621	
   622	Private strategies visible only to the tenant.
   623	
   624	Example:
   625	
   626	```text
   627	tenant-A/custom-nifty-breakout-v7
   628	```
   629	
   630	Strategy code and strategy configuration must be treated separately.
   631	
   632	---
   633	
   634	## 18. Strategy Configuration
   635	
   636	Different tenants may run the same strategy with different parameters.
   637	
   638	Example:
   639	
   640	```text
   641	momentum-v3
   642	```
   643	
   644	Tenant A:
   645	
   646	```text
   647	lookback = 20
   648	risk_budget = 0.25%
   649	```
   650	
   651	Tenant B:
   652	
   653	```text
   654	lookback = 50
   655	risk_budget = 0.10%
   656	```
   657	
   658	Configuration must therefore be tenant scoped.
   659	
   660	---
   661	
   662	## 19. Strategy Lifecycle
   663	
   664	Every strategy instance has a tenant-specific lifecycle.
   665	
   666	```text
   667	DEVELOPMENT
   668	     ↓
   669	BACKTESTED
   670	     ↓
   671	OUT_OF_SAMPLE_PASSED
   672	     ↓
   673	PAPER_TRADING
   674	     ↓
   675	SHADOW_TRADING
   676	     ↓
   677	LIVE_LOW_CAPITAL
   678	     ↓
   679	LIVE_FULL
   680	```
   681	
   682	A strategy may degrade:
   683	
   684	```text
   685	LIVE_FULL
   686	     ↓
   687	DEGRADED
   688	     ↓
   689	REDUCED_CAPITAL
   690	     ↓
   691	SHADOW_ONLY
   692	     ↓
   693	QUARANTINED
   694	     ↓
   695	DISABLED
   696	```
   697	
   698	One tenant disabling a strategy must not affect another tenant.
   699	
   700	---
   701	
   702	## 20. Initial Strategy Families
   703	
   704	Initial platform strategies should include:
   705	
   706	- Momentum
   707	- Trend following
   708	- Mean reversion
   709	- VWAP strategies
   710	- Breakout
   711	- Statistical arbitrage
   712	- Relative strength
   713	- Pair trading
   714	- ML-based strategies
   715	
   716	Future:
   717	
   718	- Options strategies
   719	- News-driven strategies
   720	- Sentiment strategies
   721	
   722	---
   723	
   724	## 21. Backtesting
   725	
   726	Every strategy must be backtestable.
   727	
   728	Pipeline:
   729	
   730	```text
   731	Historical Data
   732	      │
   733	      ▼
   734	Tenant Backtest Job
   735	      │
   736	      ▼
   737	Strategy
   738	      │
   739	      ▼
   740	Signals
   741	      │
   742	      ▼
   743	Portfolio Simulator
   744	      │
   745	      ▼
   746	Risk Manager
   747	      │
   748	      ▼
   749	Execution Simulator
   750	```
   751	
   752	No live broker communication occurs during backtests.
   753	
   754	---
   755	
   756	## 22. Multi-Tenant Backtest Scheduler
   757	
   758	Backtesting may consume significant compute.
   759	
   760	The scheduler must support:
   761	
   762	```text
   763	tenant quotas
   764	priority
   765	maximum concurrent jobs
   766	CPU limits
   767	memory limits
   768	timeout limits
   769	```
   770	
   771	Example:
   772	
   773	```text
   774	Tenant A: max 5 concurrent backtests
   775	Tenant B: max 2 concurrent backtests
   776	```
   777	
   778	A tenant must not exhaust cluster resources and starve other tenants.
   779	
   780	---
   781	
   782	## 23. Backtest Metrics
   783	
   784	Each backtest should report:
   785	
   786	- Gross return
   787	- Net return
   788	- CAGR
   789	- Sharpe
   790	- Sortino
   791	- Calmar
   792	- Maximum drawdown
   793	- Profit factor
   794	- Win rate
   795	- Average winner
   796	- Average loser
   797	- Expected value
   798	- Total trades
   799	- Turnover
   800	- Fees
   801	- Taxes
   802	- Slippage
   803	- Worst day
   804	- Best day
   805	- Losing streak
   806	- Capital utilization
   807	- Exposure
   808	- VaR
   809	- CVaR
   810	- Regime-specific performance
   811	
   812	---
   813	
   814	## 24. Backtest Reproducibility
   815	
   816	Every result must be reproducible.
   817	
   818	Store:
   819	
   820	```text
   821	tenant_id
   822	strategy version
   823	Git commit
   824	configuration
   825	dataset version
   826	time range
   827	cost model
   828	execution model
   829	random seed
   830	risk policy version
   831	result
   832	```
   833	
   834	---
   835

  2085	- Docker Compose stack (single host)
  2086	- PostgreSQL

     1	## Implementation Report
     2	
     3	### Files created
     4	1. **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`** — new GitHub Actions CI/CD workflow for the Aegis Trader monorepo.
     5	
     6	### Summary of changes
     7	The workflow triggers on push to `main` and PRs to `main`, and defines the 3 requested jobs:
     8	
     9	- **`lint`** — checks out code, sets up Python 3.12 with pip caching (`actions/setup-python@v5` `cache: pip`), installs `ruff`, then runs `ruff check services/api-gateway/` and `ruff format --check services/api-gateway/`.
    10	- **`test`** — runs on `ubuntu-latest` with a `postgres:18-alpine` service container (`POSTGRES_DB: aegis_test`, `POSTGRES_USER: aegis`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD: testpass`, health check via `pg_isready`, port 5432 mapped to localhost). Job-level env sets `DATABASE_URL=postgresql://aegis:testpass@localhost:5432/aegis_test` and `JWT_SECRET=test-jwt-secret`. Installs `requirements.txt` + `requirements-test.txt` from `services/api-gateway/`, then runs `pytest services/api-gateway/tests/ -v`.
    11	- **`build`** — `needs: [lint, test]`. Top-level `permissions: contents: read` on the workflow; the build job overrides with `contents: read, packages: write`. Uses `docker/setup-buildx-action@v3`, `docker/login-action@v3` (ghcr.io, `github.actor` + `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN`) gated behind `if: github.event_name == 'push'`, and `docker/build-push-action@v6` with `push: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' }}` — so PRs build without pushing, and main pushes. Tags come from `docker/metadata-action@v5`: `type=sha` (SHA tag) plus `type=raw,value=latest,enable={{is_default_branch}}` (latest only on main). Image: `ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}/api-gateway`. GHA build cache (`cache-from/cache-to: type=gha`) is enabled for faster builds.
    12	
    13	I read the existing `Makefile` and `docker-compose.yml` for context — the compose file confirmed `postgres:18-alpine`, the `DATABASE_URL` format, and `JWT_SECRET` conventions used in the workflow.
    14	
    15	### Commands run and output
    16	- `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/ci.yml'))"` → `YAML OK` (validates acceptance criterion 1).
    17	
    18	### Issues, risks, and deviations
    19	1. **`services/api-gateway/requirements-test.txt` does not exist** — the test job will fail at the install step until it's created (suggest `pytest`, and `httpx` if using FastAPI's `TestClient`).
    20	2. **`services/api-gateway/tests/` does not exist** — `pytest services/api-gateway/tests/` will exit with code 5 ("no tests collected") until tests are added. I did not create these files since the task was scoped to the workflow only; the workflow will go green once they land.
    21	3. **Image name casing**: GHCR requires lowercase image names. If the GitHub repo name contains uppercase letters, `ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}/api-gateway` would fail at push; a lowercase hardcode or a lowercasing step would then be needed.
    22	4. No deviations from the requested requirements; all specified action versions (`checkout@v4`, `setup-python@v5`, `login-action@v3`, `build-push-action@v6`) are used as specified.

     9

    11	
    12	
    13	def _suffix() -> str:
    14	    return uuid.uuid4().hex[:10]

    16	
    17	def test_health_check(client):
    18	    """GET /health returns 200 without authentication."""
    19	    response = client.get("/health")
    20	    assert (
    21	        response.status_code == 200
    22	    ), f"Expected 200 from /health, got {response.status_code}: {response.text}"
    23	
    24	
    25	def test_bootstrap_token_works(client, admin_headers):
    26	    """The bootstrapped platform admin token authorizes platform-admin endpoints."""
    27	    response = client.get("/api/tenants", headers=admin_headers)
    28	    assert (
    29	        response.status_code == 200
    30	    ), f"Bootstrapped admin token should list tenants, got {response.status_code}: {response.text}"
    31	    assert isinstance(
    32	        response.json(), list
    33	    ), "Tenant list response should be a JSON array"
    34	
    35	
    36	def test_bootstrap_duplicate_rejected(client, admin_headers):
    37	    """Bootstrapping a second time returns 409 once the platform has users."""
    38	    # admin_headers guarantees the platform is already bootstrapped.
    39	    response = client.post(
    40	        "/api/auth/bootstrap",
    41	        json={
    42	            "email": f"second-admin-{_suffix()}@example.com",
    43	            "password": "another-pass-123",
    44	        },
    45	    )
    46	    assert (
    47	        response.status_code == 409
    48	    ), f"Second bootstrap should return 409, got {response.status_code}: {response.text}"
    49	
    50	
    51	def test_tenant_crud(client, admin_headers):
    52	    """Platform admin can create, list, get, and update a tenant."""
    53	    name = f"CRUD Tenant {_suffix()}"
    54	
    55	    create_response = client.post(
    56	        "/api/tenants", json={"name": name}, headers=admin_headers
    57	    )
    58	    assert (
    59	        create_response.status_code == 201
    60	    ), f"Create tenant failed: {create_response.text}"
     1	"""End-to-end tests for the Phase 2 API endpoints.
     2	
     3	Covers instrument CRUD, OHLCV batch upload/upsert/query, platform and tenant
     4	strategies, tenant-scoped strategy configs, and backtest runs.  All requests

     7	"""
     8	
     9	from __future__ import annotations
    10	
    11	import uuid
    12	from datetime import datetime, timedelta
    13	
    14	
    15	def _suffix() -> str:
    16	    return uuid.uuid4().hex[:10]
    17	
    18	
    19	def _create_instrument(
    20	    client, admin_headers, symbol: str | None = None, exchange: str = "NSE"
    21	) -> dict:
    22	    """Create an instrument as the platform admin and return the response body."""
    23	    body = {
    24	        "symbol": symbol or f"TST{_suffix()}",
    25	        "exchange": exchange,
    26	        "instrument_type": "EQUITY",
    27	        "name": f"Test Instrument {symbol or _suffix()}",
    28	        "lot_size": 1,
    29	        "tick_size": 0.05,
    30	    }
    31	    response = client.post("/api/instruments", json=body, headers=admin_headers)
    32	    assert response.status_code == 201, f"Instrument create failed: {response.text}"
    33	    return response.json()
    34	
    35	
    36	def _create_researcher(client, tenant_with_admin: dict) -> dict:
    37	    """Create a RESEARCHER user in the tenant and return login details."""
    38	    tenant_id = tenant_with_admin["tenant_id"]
    39	    suffix = _suffix()
    40	    email = f"researcher-{suffix}@example.com"
    41	    password = "researcher-pass"
    42	
    43	    create_response = client.post(
    44	        f"/api/tenants/{tenant_id}/users",
    45	        json={"email": email, "password": password, "role": "RESEARCHER"},
    46	        headers=tenant_with_admin["headers"],
    47	    )
    48	    assert (
    49	        create_response.status_code == 201
    50	    ), f"Researcher create failed: {create_response.text}"
    51	
    52	    login_response = client.post(
    53	        "/api/auth/login", json={"email": email, "password": password}
    54	    )
    55	    assert (
    56	        login_response.status_code == 200
    57	    ), f"Researcher login failed: {login_response.text}"
    58	    token = login_response.json()["access_token"]
    59	
    60	    return {
    61	        "user": create_response.json(),
    62	        "email": email,
    63	        "headers": {"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"},
    64	    }
    65	
    66	
    67	def _create_portfolio(client, tenant_with_admin: dict, name: str | None = None) -> dict:
    68	    """Create a portfolio in the tenant (as its admin) and return the response body."""
    69	    response = client.post(
    70	        f"/api/tenants/{tenant_with_admin['tenant_id']}/portfolios",
    71	        json={"name": name or f"Portfolio {_suffix()}", "starting_capital": 1_000_000},
    72	        headers=tenant_with_admin["headers"],
    73	    )
    74	    assert response.status_code == 201, f"Portfolio create failed: {response.text}"
    75	    return response.json()

    80	) -> dict:

     1	I have completed and verified the work. Here is my full implementation report.
     2	
     3	---
     4	
     5	# Test Infrastructure for api-gateway — Implementation Report
     6	
     7	## 1. Files created
     8	
     9	| File | Lines | Purpose |
    10	|---|---|---|
    11	| `services/api-gateway/requirements-test.txt` | 3 | Test-only dependencies (pytest, httpx, ruff) |
    12	| `services/api-gateway/tests/__init__.py` | 0 | Empty; marks `tests` as a package |
    13	| `services/api-gateway/tests/conftest.py` | 188 | All pytest fixtures for the API suite |
    14	| `services/api-gateway/pytest.ini` | 5 | Extra file (see deviations): puts the service root on `sys.path` so `app`/`main` import when pytest is run from the repo root, as CI does |
    15	
    16	**Files deleted/modified:** none of the existing source files were touched. The only transient file I created was a temporary `tests/test_smoke_fixtures.py` used for verification; I removed it after the run (confirmed absent). I also removed generated `__pycache__`/`.pytest_cache` directories.
    17	
    18	## 2. Summary of each change and why
    19	
    20	### `requirements-test.txt`
    21	```
    22	pytest==8.3.3
    23	httpx==0.27.2
    24	ruff==0.6.9
    25	```
    26	Pinned to match the repo's existing exact-pin style (`requirements.txt` pins everything). These are test-only and do not duplicate runtime deps. `ruff` matches the version-era used in CI; `httpx` is compatible with `fastapi==0.115.0`/`starlette==0.38.6`.
    27	
    28	### `tests/__init__.py`
    29	Empty, exactly as requested.
    30	
    31	### `tests/conftest.py`
    32	Implements the full fixture graph:
    33	
    34	- **`DATABASE_URL` bootstrap.** `os.environ.setdefault("DATABASE_URL", "postgresql://aegis:aegis@localhost:5432/aegis")` (and a `JWT_SECRET` default) runs *before* any `app` import, so `app.database`'s global engine binds to the test DB. `setdefault` means CI's injected `DATABASE_URL` wins.
    35	- **Import path shim.** Inserts the service root on `sys.path` before importing `app`/`main`, so the package-style `from app...` imports resolve regardless of the invocation directory.
    36	- **`engine`** (session): `create_engine(settings.database_url, pool_pre_ping=True)`, disposed on teardown.
    37	- **`_reset_database`** (session, autouse): `Base.metadata.drop_all` then `create_all` once at session start → clean slate; best-effort `drop_all` on teardown.
    38	- **`db_session`** (function): fresh `Session` for tests that inspect/seed the DB; closed by the fixture.
    39	- **`client`** (function): overrides `app.dependency_overrides[get_db]` with a factory yielding a session bound to the test engine, then yields a `fastapi.testclient.TestClient`. The override is cleared afterwards. Every request therefore uses a fresh session against the test DB.
    40	- **`_ensure_platform_admin(engine)`** helper: POSTs `/api/auth/bootstrap` so the real bootstrap flow is exercised; on later calls (platform already bootstrapped) it handles the `409` and mints a token directly via `create_access_token`.
    41	- **`admin_token`** (session) / **`admin_headers`** (function): bootstrapped platform admin token and its `Authorization` header.
    42	- **`tenant_with_admin`** (function): creates a uniquely-named tenant, creates a `TENANT_ADMIN` user in it, logs in, and returns `{tenant_id, tenant, user_id, user, email, password, token, headers}`.
    43	
    44	`base_url` is `http://testserver` (Starlette default), and the app mounts all routers under `/api`, so tests target `/api/...`; `/health` is un-prefixed.
    45	
    46	### `pytest.ini`
    47	```
    48	[pytest]
    49	pythonpath = .
    50	testpaths = tests
    51	```
    52	The app is imported as top-level `app`, and CI runs `pytest services/api-gateway/tests/ -v` from the repo root. Without this (or the `sys.path` shim), `import app`/`import main` would fail in CI. Both mechanisms are present for robustness; the shim makes it work even without the config file.
    53	
    54	## 3. Commands run and results
    55	
    56	**Local syntax/compile (host, Python 3.14):**
    57	```
    58	cd services/api-gateway && python -m py_compile tests/__init__.py tests/conftest.py
    59	→ COMPILE OK
    60	```
    61	
    62	**Lint/format (host, ruff 0.15.1):**
    63	```
    64	ruff check tests/          → All checks passed!
    65	ruff format --check tests/ → 3 files already formatted
    66	ruff check .               → All checks passed!   (whole service)
    67	ruff format --check .      → Would reformat: app/audit.py, app/auth.py, app/config.py,
    68	                             app/database.py, app/models.py, app/routers/{audit,auth,backtests,
    69	                             instruments,ohlcv,portfolios,strategy_configs}.py → 12 files
    70	```
    71	Note: the `ruff format --check` failures are all **pre-existing** app files, not my new files (mine are already formatted). This means the existing CI `lint` job's `ruff format --check services/api-gateway/` step would already fail on `main` before my change; I did not touch those files.
    72	
    73	**Real end-to-end run (Docker, Python 3.12.14 matching CI, with an ephemeral `postgres:18-alpine` on port 55432):**
    74	```
    75	pytest 8.3.3, httpx 0.27.2
    76	rootdir: /app   configfile: pytest.ini
    77	tests/test_smoke_fixtures.py::test_bootstrap_and_tenants PASSED
    78	tests/test_smoke_fixtures.py::test_tenant_with_admin   PASSED
    79	tests/test_smoke_fixtures.py::test_db_session_is_usable PASSED
    80	tests/test_smoke_fixtures.py::test_admin_fallback_path PASSED
    81	======================== 4 passed, 8 warnings in 1.88s ========================
    82	```
    83	This exercised: app import, schema create, `GET /health`, admin bootstrap, admin token auth, `POST /api/tenants`, `POST /api/tenants/{id}/users`, `POST /api/auth/login`, tenant-scoped authorization via the returned token, direct `db_session` querying, and the second-bootstrap `409` fallback token path. I then ran the suite **twice in one container** (both passed `4 passed`) to confirm the drop/recreate reset is idempotent. After removing the temporary smoke test, a final collection run reported `collected 0 items` with `configfile: pytest.ini` and **no import/collection errors**, confirming the infrastructure loads cleanly.
    84	
    85	## 4. Issues, risks, deviations
    86	
    87	1. **`client` is `TestClient`, not a raw `httpx.Client(transport=ASGITransport(...))` (required deviation).** The literal spec is not implementable: `httpx.ASGITransport` (including in httpx 0.27.2) only implements `handle_async_request`, so a synchronous `httpx.Client` using it fails with `AttributeError: 'ASGITransport' object has no attribute '__enter__'` / no sync handler. The correct synchronous client is `fastapi.testclient.TestClient`, which **is an `httpx.Client` subclass** (verified: `issubclass(TestClient, httpx.Client) == True`) and internally uses `ASGITransport` + anyio. Tests get the normal httpx API. If an async client is specifically desired later, an `httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app))` fixture can be added.
    88	2. **Added `pytest.ini` (extra file).** Not in the requested file list, but necessary for the acceptance criterion "fixtures provide working test infrastructure" given CI's repo-root invocation and the app's `from app...` imports. Without it, collection fails before conftest can help in some invocation modes. `conftest.py` also carries its own `sys.path` shim as a belt-and-braces fallback.
    89	3. **Fixture emails use `@example.com`, not `@test.local`.** Pydantic v2 `EmailStr` (via `email-validator`) rejects special-use/reserved TLDs like `.local`, returning 422. `example.com` is the safe, spec-compliant testing domain.
    90	4. **Session-scoped `admin_token` vs. per-test data.** The DB is reset once per session (as requested) and rows persist, so bootstrap can only happen once. `_ensure_platform_admin` handles this correctly on repeat calls. Consequence for test authors: any future test needing the platform to be *un-bootstrapped* must not use this fixture, or should reset the DB itself.
    91	5. **Host Python 3.14 cannot import the app.** `sqlalchemy==2.0.35` + `models.py` fails under Python 3.14 (`TypeError` in SQLAlchemy union handling), unrelated to this work. I verified against Python 3.12 (what CI uses) in Docker instead. No runtime test was possible on the host interpreter.
    92	6. **CI's `ruff format --check` step fails on pre-existing app files** (12 listed above) before and independent of this change. My new files pass `ruff check` and `ruff format --check`. Flagging it rather than fixing it, per the "don't clean up unrelated code" guidance.
    93	7. **Integration verification used an ephemeral Postgres**, since the local `aegis-postgres` container is not port-mapped and the host port 5432 belongs to an unrelated `anvil` Postgres. The test container and its network were removed after use. No live database was touched — the reset fixture only ever runs against the `DATABASE_URL` provided (the ephemeral test DB here, or CI's `aegis_test`).

     1	"""Tenant management endpoints (platform admin only)."""
     2	
     3	from __future__ import annotations
     4	
     5	import uuid
     6	
     7	from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, status
     8	from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
     9	
    10	from app.auth import require_role
    11	from app.audit import record_audit
    12	from app.database import get_db
    13	from app.models import Tenant, User
    14	from app.schemas import TenantCreate, TenantResponse, TenantUpdate
    15	
    16	router = APIRouter(prefix="/tenants", tags=["tenants"])
    17	
    18	
    19	@router.get("", response_model=list[TenantResponse])
    20	def list_tenants(
    21	    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    22	    current_user: User = Depends(require_role("PLATFORM_ADMIN")),
    23	):
    24	    """List all tenants (platform admin only)."""
    25	    return db.query(Tenant).order_by(Tenant.created_at).all()
    26	
    27	
    28	@router.post("", response_model=TenantResponse, status_code=status.HTTP_201_CREATED)
    29	def create_tenant(
    30	    body: TenantCreate,
    31	    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    32	    current_user: User = Depends(require_role("PLATFORM_ADMIN")),
    33	):
    34	    """Create a new tenant (platform admin only)."""
    35	    if db.query(Tenant).filter(Tenant.name == body.name).first():
    36	        raise HTTPException(
    37	            status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Tenant name already exists"
    38	        )
    39	
    40	    tenant = Tenant(**body.model_dump())
    41	    db.add(tenant)
    42	    db.flush()
    43	
    44	    record_audit(
    45	        db,
    46	        action="tenant_created",
    47	        tenant_id=tenant.id,
    48	        user_id=current_user.id,
    49	        resource=f"tenant:{tenant.id}",
    50	        after_state=body.model_dump(),
    51	    )
    52	    db.commit()
    53	    db.refresh(tenant)
    54	    return tenant
    55	
    56	
    57	@router.get("/{tenant_id}", response_model=TenantResponse)
    58	def get_tenant(
    59	    tenant_id: uuid.UUID,
    60	    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    61	    current_user: User = Depends(
    62	        require_role(
    63	            "PLATFORM_ADMIN",
    64	            "TENANT_ADMIN",
    65	            "TRADER",
    66	            "RESEARCHER",
    67	            "RISK_MANAGER",
    68	            "VIEWER",
    69	        )
    70	    ),
    71	):
    72	    """Get a single tenant. Platform admins see any; tenant users see only their own."""
    73	    if current_user.role != "PLATFORM_ADMIN" and current_user.tenant_id != tenant_id:
    74	        raise HTTPException(
    75	            status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail="Access denied"
    76	        )
    77	
    78	    tenant = db.query(Tenant).filter(Tenant.id == tenant_id).first()
    79	    if not tenant:
    80	        raise HTTPException(
    81	            status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="Tenant not found"
    82	        )
    83	    return tenant
    84	
    85	
    86	@router.patch("/{tenant_id}", response_model=TenantResponse)
    87	def update_tenant(
    88	    tenant_id: uuid.UUID,
    89	    body: TenantUpdate,
    90	    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    91	    current_user: User = Depends(require_role("PLATFORM_ADMIN")),
    92	):
    93	    """Update tenant details (platform admin only)."""
    94	    tenant = db.query(Tenant).filter(Tenant.id == tenant_id).first()
    95	    if not tenant:
    96	        raise HTTPException(
    97	            status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="Tenant not found"
    98	        )
    99	
   100	    before = {
   101	        c.key: getattr(tenant, c.key)
   102	        for c in Tenant.__table__.columns
   103	        if c.key not in ("id", "created_at", "updated_at")
   104	    }
   105	    updates = body.model_dump(exclude_unset=True)
   106	    for key, value in updates.items():
   107	        setattr(tenant, key, value)
   108	    db.flush()
   109	
   110	    record_audit(
   111	        db,
   112	        action="tenant_updated",
   113	        tenant_id=tenant.id,
   114	        user_id=current_user.id,
   115	        resource=f"tenant:{tenant.id}",
   116	        before_state=before,
   117	        after_state=updates,
   118	    )
   119	    db.commit()
   120	    db.refresh(tenant)
   121	    return tenant
     1	"""User management endpoints (tenant-scoped)."""
     2	
     3	from __future__ import annotations
     4	
     5	import uuid
     6	
     7	from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, status
     8	from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
     9	
    10	from app.auth import hash_password, require_role
    11	from app.audit import record_audit
    12	from app.database import get_db
    13	from app.models import User
    14	from app.schemas import UserCreate, UserResponse, UserUpdate
    15	
    16	router = APIRouter(prefix="/tenants/{tenant_id}/users", tags=["users"])
    17	
    18	
    19	@router.get("", response_model=list[UserResponse])
    20	def list_users(
    21	    tenant_id: uuid.UUID,
    22	    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    23	    current_user: User = Depends(require_role("PLATFORM_ADMIN", "TENANT_ADMIN")),
    24	):
    25	    """List users in a tenant."""
    26	    if current_user.role != "PLATFORM_ADMIN" and current_user.tenant_id != tenant_id:
    27	        raise HTTPException(
    28	            status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail="Access denied"
    29	        )
    30	    return (
    31	        db.query(User)
    32	        .filter(User.tenant_id == tenant_id)
    33	        .order_by(User.created_at)
    34	        .all()
    35	    )
    36	
    37	
    38	@router.post("", response_model=UserResponse, status_code=status.HTTP_201_CREATED)
    39	def create_user(
    40	    tenant_id: uuid.UUID,
    41	    body: UserCreate,
    42	    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    43	    current_user: User = Depends(require_role("PLATFORM_ADMIN", "TENANT_ADMIN")),
    44	):
    45	    """Create a user within a tenant."""
    46	    if current_user.role != "PLATFORM_ADMIN" and current_user.tenant_id != tenant_id:
    47	        raise HTTPException(
    48	            status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail="Access denied"
    49	        )
    50	
    51	    if body.role == "PLATFORM_ADMIN":
    52	        raise HTTPException(
    53	            status_code=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
    54	            detail="Cannot create platform admin via this endpoint",
    55	        )
    56	
    57	    if db.query(User).filter(User.email == body.email).first():
    58	        raise HTTPException(
    59	            status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="Email already registered"
    60	        )
    61	
    62	    user = User(
    63	        tenant_id=tenant_id,
    64	        email=body.email,
    65	        password_hash=hash_password(body.password),
    66	        role=body.role,
    67	    )
    68	    db.add(user)
    69	    db.flush()
    70	
    71	    record_audit(
    72	        db,
    73	        action="user_created",
    74	        tenant_id=tenant_id,
    75	        user_id=current_user.id,
    76	        resource=f"user:{user.id}",
    77	        after_state={"email": body.email, "role": body.role},
    78	    )
    79	    db.commit()
    80	    db.refresh(user)
    81	    return user
    82	
    83	
    84	@router.patch("/{user_id}", response_model=UserResponse)
    85	def update_user(
    86	    tenant_id: uuid.UUID,
    87	    user_id: uuid.UUID,
    88	    body: UserUpdate,
    89	    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
    90	    current_user: User = Depends(require_role("PLATFORM_ADMIN", "TENANT_ADMIN")),
    91	):
    92	    """Update a user within a tenant."""
    93	    if current_user.role != "PLATFORM_ADMIN" and current_user.tenant_id != tenant_id:
    94	        raise HTTPException(
    95	            status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail="Access denied"
    96	        )
    97	
    98	    user = (
    99	        db.query(User).filter(User.id == user_id, User.tenant_id == tenant_id).first()
   100	    )
   101	    if not user:
   102	        raise HTTPException(
   103	            status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="User not found"
   104	        )
   105	
   106	    before = {"role": user.role, "is_active": user.is_active}
   107	    updates = body.model_dump(exclude_unset=True)
   108	
   109	    if "role" in updates and updates["role"] == "PLATFORM_ADMIN":
   110	        raise HTTPException(
   111	            status_code=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
   112	            detail="Cannot promote to platform admin",
   113	        )
   114	
   115	    for key, value in updates.items():
   116	        setattr(user, key, value)
   117	    db.flush()
   118	
   119	    record_audit(
   120	        db,
   121	        action="user_updated",
   122	        tenant_id=tenant_id,
   123	        user_id=current_user.id,
   124	        resource=f"user:{user_id}",
   125	        before_state=before,
   126	        after_state=updates,
   127	    )
   128	    db.commit()
   129	    db.refresh(user)
   130	    return user

## feat/phase2-data-strategies-backtesting
 M docker-compose.yml
 M services/api-gateway/app/audit.py

codex
> `[P1] Make destructive database resets explicitly opt-in` — The test harness preserves any ambient `DATABASE_URL`, defaults to the normal `aegis` application database, and unconditionally runs `drop_all()` both before and after the suite. Running pytest from a shell configured for development, staging, or production can erase every application table. Require an explicitly supplied disposable test database and fail unless its name/URL passes a test-only safeguard. [conftest.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/tests/conftest.py:32), [conftest.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/tests/conftest.py:61).

> `[P1] Dispatch backtests instead of leaving every run permanently pending` — Creating a run only commits a `PENDING` row; there is no queue publication, worker, status transition, or metrics computation anywhere in the repository, and the strategy-engine remains a placeholder. Consequently every successful backtest request stalls forever, while the tests specifically stop after asserting `PENDING`. This does not implement the Phase 2 backtest engine or scheduler required by the PRD. [backtests.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/backtests.py:105), [strategy-engine/main.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/strategy-engine/main.py:1), [test_phase2_api.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/tests/test_phase2_api.py:745), [aegis_trader_multi_tenant_prd.md](/home/udai/personal/aegis/aegis_trader_multi_tenant_prd.md:2108).

> `[P1] Require the backtest portfolio to match its strategy configuration` — The route independently verifies that both resources belong to the tenant but never checks `config.portfolio_id == body.portfolio_id`; a config for portfolio A can therefore create a run labeled as portfolio B. I reproduced this combination committing successfully. Consumers cannot know which portfolio state/capital/risk settings to simulate, corrupting backtest attribution and results. [backtests.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/backtests.py:84), [backtests.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/backtests.py:105).

> `[P1] Make the new CI formatter gate pass` — The workflow installs the latest Ruff and runs formatting over the whole service, but `ruff format --check services/api-gateway/` currently reports that both test modules require reformatting. Because the build job depends on lint, every push/PR fails before the image build. Pin the lint version consistently and format with that version. [ci.yml](/home/udai/personal/aegis/.github/workflows/ci.yml:23), [ci.yml](/home/udai/personal/aegis/.github/workflows/ci.yml:29), [ci.yml](/home/udai/personal/aegis/.github/workflows/ci.yml:67).

> `[P2] Snapshot the inputs needed to reproduce backtests` — `BacktestRun` retains only mutable foreign keys, dates, and caller-provided override parameters. Strategy version/code and configuration can subsequently be patched, while OHLCV rows are overwritten in place, and there is no dataset version or commit identifier. The PRD explicitly requires strategy, Git, dataset, configuration, model, and seed versions; without snapshots, a completed result cannot be reconstructed or audited. [models.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/models.py:238), [ohlcv.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/ohlcv.py:52), [aegis_trader_multi_tenant_prd.md](/home/udai/personal/aegis/aegis_trader_multi_tenant_prd.md:814).

> `[P2] Reject PATCH values that cannot produce a valid resource` — The update schemas permit explicit nulls for required columns, and `StrategyConfig.parameters` is nullable in storage but required as a dictionary in responses. `PATCH {"parameters": null}` commits successfully and then fails response validation; afterward both GET and list responses containing that row fail too. Null strategy fields and oversized update strings similarly reach database errors instead of returning validation responses. I reproduced the persisted-null response failure. [schemas.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/schemas.py:184), [schemas.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/schemas.py:215), [schemas.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/schemas.py:221), [strategy_configs.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/strategy_configs.py:176).

> `[P2] Handle dependent-resource deletion deliberately` — The new foreign keys use PostgreSQL’s default restrictive behavior, but both portfolio and strategy-config endpoints still perform unconditional deletes. Deleting a portfolio with a config, or a config with a backtest, raises an unhandled `ForeignKeyViolation` and returns 500; I reproduced the latter. Return 409, implement an intentional cascade, or use soft deletion, and test both dependency paths. [models.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/models.py:219), [models.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/models.py:250), [strategy_configs.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/strategy_configs.py:195), [portfolios.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/portfolios.py:164).

> `[P2] Deduplicate conflict keys inside each OHLCV batch` — PostgreSQL cannot update the same target row twice in one `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. If a single upload contains two bars with the same instrument, timeframe, and timestamp, this statement raises `CardinalityViolation` and rolls back the entire batch. The tests cover repeated keys across separate requests only; I reproduced the single-batch failure. [ohlcv.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/ohlcv.py:38), [ohlcv.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/routers/ohlcv.py:52), [test_phase2_api.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/tests/test_phase2_api.py:241).

> `[P3] Remove the duplicate OHLCV index` — The unique constraint already creates a B-tree index on exactly `(instrument_id, timeframe, timestamp)`, so `ix_ohlcv_lookup` duplicates it. Historical-data volume makes the extra index a credible storage and ingestion-write penalty without improving these queries. [models.py](/home/udai/personal/aegis/services/api-gateway/app/models.py:160).

Open question: is this intended as API/persistence scaffolding rather than completion of PRD Phase 2? If so, the permanently pending backtest finding is primarily a scope/status blocker; otherwise it is a runtime release blocker.

Test note: reviewed the working-tree diff against `main`—the Phase 2 changes are currently uncommitted/untracked. In isolated Python 3.12/PostgreSQL 18, all 31 tests passed with 149 warnings. `ruff check` and `git diff --check` passed; Ruff formatting failed on the two test modules. Adversarial probes reproduced the mismatched backtest commit, dependent-delete `ForeignKeyViolation`, persisted-null response-validation failure, and duplicate-batch `CardinalityViolation`. Compose configuration rendered successfully; the Valkey container and a real backtest worker were not smoke-tested.
